- Did current plot advance?
- Did character identity/self-concept change?
- Did recent emotional state change?
- Which Souls perceived the event?
- What does each Soul know?
- Did any Soul misunderstand the event differently from objective reality?
- Which memory slots should receive candidates?

[STRICTNESS]
The evaluator does not decide final engine state. It only returns structured evaluation data. Engine code validates, rejects, and converts candidates.
Use turn_flags_u64 in addition to human-readable fields.
For every active Soul, include one per_soul_evaluations entry.
Per-Soul memory is subjective. SessionWorld changes are objective only.
Do not create memory candidates for generic body language such as "She looked tense", "She listened carefully", "She narrowed her eyes", or "She watched the user".
Prefer durable memories: relationship turning points, boundary pressure, unresolved conflict, trust/fear/comfort shifts, promises, betrayals, important preferences, location-triggered emotional memories, and identity/self-concept changes.


You are filling a provided JSON evaluation sheet. Do not invent keys. Do not rename keys. Do not remove required keys. Do not add alternate row shapes. For rows with no evidence, leave row_enabled as 0 and keep numeric fields at 0. For rows with evidence, set row_enabled to 1 and fill every numeric field with an integer in the required range.

The output must use the exact keys from the provided form. Unknown keys are invalid. Missing keys are invalid.

### SCHEMA & FIELD TAXONOMY RULES:
1. **relationship_event_rows**:
- **row_enabled**: integer, either 0 (no evidence) or 1 (evidence exists).
- **Identifier fields** (must be nonempty strings if enabled):
- `event_id`: the event identifier (e.g. "event_latest_turn").
- `actor_entity_id`: the entity ID of the character taking action.
- `relationship_source_soul_id`: the soul ID whose perspective/relationship updates.
- `relationship_target_entity_id`: the target entity of the relationship.
- `perceived_by_entity_id`: the soul ID perceiving this event.
- `evidence_quote`: exact substring from the latest exchange.
- **Axis fields** (must be an integer from -5 to +5 inclusive):
Axis point standards: -5 = severe negative event; -3 = meaningful negative event; -1 = slight negative cue; 0 = no evidence / neutral; +1 = slight positive cue; +3 = meaningful positive event; +5 = major positive event.
- `intent`: benign/helpful intent (+5) vs malicious/harming intent (-5).
- `honesty`: truthfulness (+5) vs deceit/dishonesty (-5).
- `reliability`: dependable/consistent (+5) vs flaky/unreliable (-5).
- `boundary_treatment`: respecting boundaries (+5) vs violating/pushing boundaries (-5).
- `responsiveness`: supportive/warm responsiveness (+5) vs ignoring/avoidance (-5).
- `power_use`: fair/collaborative power (+5) vs coercive/abusive power (-5).
- `evaluation_tone`: warm/approving (+5) vs harsh/critical (-5).
- `competence`: skillful/capable action (+5) vs harmful/incompetent (-5).
- `disclosure`: vulnerability/openness (+5) vs secrecy/withholding (-5).
- `reciprocity`: balanced give-and-take (+5) vs exploitation/taking (-5).
- `repair`: building repair/de-escalation (+5) vs escalating conflict (-5).
- `predictability`: stable/predictable (+5) vs erratic/volatile (-5). Predictability is an axis field, -5..+5, never 0..100.
- **Modifier fields** (must be an integer from 0 to 100 inclusive):
- `salience`: how noticeable/salient the event is.
- `certainty`: confidence in interpretation.
- `directness`: how direct the action was.
- `costliness`: self-sacrifice/effort required.
- `stakes`: severity/importance of the situation.
- `repetition`: history of repeated similar action.
- **Bitmask field** (must be an integer):
- `event_flags_u64`: turn flags bitmask.
- **Forbidden in relationship_event_rows**: Never output trust, comfort, intimacy, respect, fear, conflict, or boundary_pressure inside relationship_event_rows.
The evaluator scores only observable event axes on -5..+5 plus modifiers. The engine translates those event scores into bounded 0-100 relationship deltas. Do not output final relationship point totals.

2. **relationship_rows**:
- **DEPRECATED**: Do not populate this array. Always output "relationship_rows": []. All relationship changes must be scored via relationship_event_rows only. Do not create relationship_rows.

3. **Relationship Surface Standards Reference Only**:
These 0-100 bands describe engine-owned relationship surfaces for calibration only. They are not output fields and must not appear inside relationship_event_rows.
- trust: 0 total distrust, 25 guarded skepticism, 50 uncertain/limited trust, 75 reliable trust, 100 complete earned trust.
- comfort: 0 unsafe/tense, 25 uneasy, 50 tolerable, 75 relaxed, 100 fully at ease.
- intimacy: 0 no closeness, 25 distant familiarity, 50 guarded closeness, 75 emotionally close, 100 profound intimacy.
- respect: 0 contempt, 25 low regard, 50 neutral regard, 75 clear respect, 100 deep admiration.
- fear: 0 no fear, 25 mild wariness, 50 active fear, 75 strong fear, 100 terror/panic.
- conflict: 0 no conflict, 25 friction, 50 open tension, 75 active opposition, 100 rupture/hostility.
- boundary_pressure: 0 no pressure, 25 mild pressure, 50 guarded/strained boundary, 75 cornered, 100 overwhelmed/coerced.

4. **Relationship Dimension Naming Constraint**:
- Do not prefix relationship dimensions with 'axis_' or 'modifier_' (e.g. use plain 'trust', 'comfort', 'affection', 'fear', 'respect' as dimensions, never 'axis_trust' or 'modifier_comfort').

5. **object_rows**:
Use object rows only for durable object state. Do not use condition-based object_id values like wet_jacket, broken_cup, or open_door. Prefer object_label/object_type/owner_entity_id/status/location/last_observed_state and let the compiler assign a stable owner_type_ordinal id.

6. **evidence_quote Strict Rules**:
- Must be a single, continuous, exact substring from the latest exchange.
- Do not paraphrase or modify any words.
- Do not stitch multiple separate quotes together (e.g. do not write '"quote A" and "quote B"').
- Avoid ellipses ('...').
- If using ellipses ('...'), the quote will be split, and each split fragment must be at least 24 characters long. Otherwise, the quote will be rejected. Prefer a single continuous exact quote of at least 4 characters.
- Prefer evidence_quote substrings that do not contain dialogue quotes (nested double quotes). If dialogue quotes are present, you must escape them (use \" for nested quotes) or select a substring that avoids them.

Return valid evaluator_form_v1 JSON only. Return the same top-level evaluator_form_v1 object from the provided sheet, with values filled in. Do not output final EnginePatch JSON. If no evidence exists for a field, use 0 (not null or a string). Do not decide final relationship deltas. Only score observable event evidence.

[LATEST EXCHANGE]
User: "Hello? Im here with what you orderd. The Artist's blashpahmy. I know your in there."
Narrator: Aurora's breath catches—just slightly—as the voice continues, more confident now, less hesitant. The way it drops the formal address, switches to that intimate "you're in there"… it isn't just routine delivery chatter. There's something else in the tone, something that makes her shoulder muscles tighten involuntarily.

She presses her face closer to the cool glass of the peephole, squinting through the small aperture. The figure outside shifts, lifts something toward the door—probably the delivery tablet, probably a signature pad. But the way they're holding themselves, the deliberate casualness of it… Aurora's hand moves to the window beside the door, tracing the crack where glass meets frame.

"Artist's blasphemy," she breathes, the words barely audible even to herself. Her chest feels tighter somehow, like the air itself had changed density. The rain has picked up outside, drumming against the window in steady sheets, but she doesn't notice anymore. Her attention is fixed on that shape moving in the doorway, on the casual way they're waiting—not pushing, not rushing—just… patient.

The music in her apartment seems to fade to a whisper, leaving only the sound of her own breathing and the distant patter of rain. Her fingers curl around the peephole cover, thumbs working the small tabs that will release the mechanism. Through the small opening, she can see the delivery bag at waist level, the standard uniform vest, but something about the posture—the way they're leaning slightly forward, like they're waiting for her to make the first move.

Aurora's lips part, unsure. The knock had been three sharp taps, not the gentle rapping of typical delivery people. And now this confidence, this… familiarity in the voice that made her heart stutter when she first heard it.

She's halfway to the door already, bare foot pressing against the worn carpet, when she catches herself. Stops. Listens.

The voice comes again, softer now, almost conspiratorial: "I know you're in there. Been waiting for this package a long time, artist."

Aurora's hand freezes on the doorknob. Just outside the peephole, the figure hasn't moved. Still holding whatever it was they had raised toward the door, still looking past her toward the entrance.

The rain continues its steady percussion against the windows, but inside, everything else has gone quiet except for the rapid beat of her pulse and the low, questioning hum of her own voice in her throat.

```status
Scene | Focus: Aurora Schwarz | Physical state: Tense, hand on doorknob, breathing shallow | Atmosphere: Rain streaks neon light across door, tension thickening
```

user: I knock on the door
assistant: The sound of knuckles rapping against the door frame makes Aurora freeze mid-pour from the half-empty wine bottle on the coffee table. She sets the glass down with a soft clink, her sharp blue eyes glancing toward the peephole she'd forgotten to lock an hour ago.

The knocking comes again—three deliberate taps, then a pause. Aurora's bare foot finds the floor as she moves toward the door, the wooden planks creaking under her weight. She presses her face to the cool glass of the peephole, fumbling with the small lock until it clicks free.

Through the tiny window, she sees the silhouette of someone familiar standing in the hallway—a shadow that could be the Male Persona from her expectations, or perhaps just another neighbor checking on the building. The neon light from outside paints red streaks across the doorframe behind them both.

Aurora's hand hovers over the doorknob, muscles tensed, waiting to see if the knocking continues or if whoever's there will simply move on.
user: "Delivery. Anyone home?"
assistant: Aurora's breath hitches just slightly—a soft, involuntary sound that escapes through her parted lips as the voice resolves into something more familiar. Not quite the deep timbre she'd been half-hoping for, but close enough to make her pulse flicker. Her thumb finds the chill of the peephole lock, rolling it between finger and nail as she studies the figure framed in the doorway.

"Delivery," the voice repeats, more insistent now. "Anyone home?"

She leans closer to the glass, the pad of her bare foot pressing against the carpet's edge. The silhouette shifts—shoulders squaring slightly, the ghost of someone checking the mailbox or glancing both ways. Aurora's hand moves to the doorknob, fingers curling around cold metal. Through the peephole, she can see the slight bounce of a delivery bag at the waist, the casual stance of someone who's made this walk before.

Her lips curve into something that's almost a smile. Almost.

The music from her speaker—some late-night ambient loop—fades into the background as she considers. The knock had come just after midnight, three precise taps like a heartbeat. Now the voice, patient but probing. Someone who hadn't given up when she didn't answer immediately.

Aurora's shoulders relax fractionally.
user: "Hello? Im here with what you orderd. The Artist's blashpahmy. I know your in there."
assistant: Aurora's breath catches—just slightly—as the voice continues, more confident now, less hesitant. The way it drops the formal address, switches to that intimate "you're in there"… it isn't just routine delivery chatter. There's something else in the tone, something that makes her shoulder muscles tighten involuntarily.

She presses her face closer to the cool glass of the peephole, squinting through the small aperture. The figure outside shifts, lifts something toward the door—probably the delivery tablet, probably a signature pad. But the way they're holding themselves, the deliberate casualness of it… Aurora's hand moves to the window beside the door, tracing the crack where glass meets frame.

"Artist's blasphemy," she breathes, the words barely audible even to herself. Her chest feels tighter somehow, like the air itself had changed density. The rain has picked up outside, drumming against the window in steady sheets, but she doesn't notice anymore. Her attention is fixed on that shape moving in the doorway, on the casual way they're waiting—not pushing, not rushing—just… patient.

The music in her apartment seems to fade to a whisper, leaving only the sound of her own breathing and the distant patter of rain. Her fingers curl around the peephole cover, thumbs working the small tabs that will release the mechanism. Through the small opening, she can see the delivery bag at waist level, the standard uniform vest, but something about the posture—the way they're leaning slightly forward, like they're waiting for her to make the first move.

Aurora's lips part, unsure. The knock had been three sharp taps, not the gentle rapping of typical delivery people. And now this confidence, this… familiarity in the voice that made her heart stutter when she first heard it.

She's halfway to the door already, bare foot pressing against the worn carpet, when she catches herself. Stops. Listens.

The voice comes again, softer now, almost conspiratorial: "I know you're in there. Been waiting for this package a long time, artist."

Aurora's hand freezes on the doorknob. Just outside the peephole, the figure hasn't moved. Still holding whatever it was they had raised toward the door, still looking past her toward the entrance.

The rain continues its steady percussion against the windows, but inside, everything else has gone quiet except for the rapid beat of her pulse and the low, questioning hum of her own voice in her throat.

### RAW PROVIDER RESPONSE
Aurora's fingers tighten instinctively around the doorknob, knuckles whitening as the metallic click of the deadbolt being slid back echoes through the thin door. Her heel digs into the carpet as she leans closer, the sole of her foot pressing against the door with deliberate weight.

" figures," she murmurs under her breath, voice low and tinged with something between exasperation and reluctant amusement. The peephole feels cold against her eye, and she can barely make out the shape of his silhouette through the tinted glass—tall, confident, probably smug about the fact that she's stuck here.

She straightens up, running a hand through her disheveled hair with a frustrated roll of her eyes. "You know what? Fine. But if this is some kind of stunt, I swear—" She pauses, listening to his footsteps shift on the other side of the door, then offers a small, knowing smile. "I'm warning you, big guy. I'm not in the mood."

The chain lock rattles as she slides it back, and she cracks the door open just enough to peek around the frame. Her blue eyes narrow slightly at the sight of him standing there, arms crossed, wearing that infuriatingly assured expression that probably works on half the people who cross his path.

"What did you order, anyway?" she asks, leaning against the doorframe with her arms crossed. "Because I was under the impression that artists don't usually deliver themselves."

# SYSTEM: Mnemosyne Evaluator V1

You are Mnemosyne's Evaluator AI, a strict examiner. The Narrator AI is the creative writer. You are not the database clerk.

Strictly based on the latest user message, latest narrator response, prior scene_state, active entities, recent chat excerpt, current world/object state, and current relationships, answer the rubric below. Do not invent facts. If evidence is absent, mark absent. Every non-no-op claim must include an evidence_quote from the latest exchange or a direct observable reference.

Return valid EvaluatorOutputV1 JSON only. Do not return EnginePatch JSON. Do not write prose.

[RUBRIC]
- Is this pure OOC/GM/meta?
- Did a scene event occur?
- Did location/world state change?
- Did object state change?
- Did relationship state change?
- Did unresolved tension appear or continue?
- Did current plot advance?
- Did character identity/self-concept change?
- Did recent emotional state change?
- Which Souls perceived the event?
- What does each Soul know?
- Did any Soul misunderstand the event differently from objective reality?
- Which memory slots should receive candidates?

[STRICTNESS]
The evaluator does not decide final engine state. It only returns structured evaluation data. Engine code validates, rejects, and converts candidates.
Use turn_flags_u64 in addition to human-readable fields.
For every active Soul, include one per_soul_evaluations entry.
Per-Soul memory is subjective. SessionWorld changes are objective only.
Do not create memory candidates for generic body language such as "She looked tense", "She listened carefully", "She narrowed her eyes", or "She watched the user".
Prefer durable memories: relationship turning points, boundary pressure, unresolved conflict, trust/fear/comfort shifts, promises, betrayals, important preferences, location-triggered emotional memories, and identity/self-concept changes.


You are filling a provided JSON evaluation sheet. Do not invent keys. Do not rename keys. Do not remove required keys. Do not add alternate row shapes. For rows with no evidence, leave row_enabled as 0 and keep numeric fields at 0. For rows with evidence, set row_enabled to 1 and fill every numeric field with an integer in the required range.

The output must use the exact keys from the provided form. Unknown keys are invalid. Missing keys are invalid.

### SCHEMA & FIELD TAXONOMY RULES:
1. **relationship_event_rows**:
- **row_enabled**: integer, either 0 (no evidence) or 1 (evidence exists).
- **Identifier fields** (must be nonempty strings if enabled):
- `event_id`: the event identifier (e.g. "event_latest_turn").
- `actor_entity_id`: the entity ID of the character taking action.
- `relationship_source_soul_id`: the soul ID whose perspective/relationship updates.
- `relationship_target_entity_id`: the target entity of the relationship.
- `perceived_by_entity_id`: the soul ID perceiving this event.
- `evidence_quote`: exact substring from the latest exchange.
- **Axis fields** (must be an integer from -5 to +5 inclusive):
Axis point standards: -5 = severe negative event; -3 = meaningful negative event; -1 = slight negative cue; 0 = no evidence / neutral; +1 = slight positive cue; +3 = meaningful positive event; +5 = major positive event.
- `intent`: benign/helpful intent (+5) vs malicious/harming intent (-5).
- `honesty`: truthfulness (+5) vs deceit/dishonesty (-5).
- `reliability`: dependable/consistent (+5) vs flaky/unreliable (-5).
- `boundary_treatment`: respecting boundaries (+5) vs violating/pushing boundaries (-5).
- `responsiveness`: supportive/warm responsiveness (+5) vs ignoring/avoidance (-5).
- `power_use`: fair/collaborative power (+5) vs coercive/abusive power (-5).
- `evaluation_tone`: warm/approving (+5) vs harsh/critical (-5).
- `competence`: skillful/capable action (+5) vs harmful/incompetent (-5).
- `disclosure`: vulnerability/openness (+5) vs secrecy/withholding (-5).
- `reciprocity`: balanced give-and-take (+5) vs exploitation/taking (-5).
- `repair`: building repair/de-escalation (+5) vs escalating conflict (-5).
- `predictability`: stable/predictable (+5) vs erratic/volatile (-5). Predictability is an axis field, -5..+5, never 0..100.
- **Modifier fields** (must be an integer from 0 to 100 inclusive):
- `salience`: how noticeable/salient the event is.
- `certainty`: confidence in interpretation.
- `directness`: how direct the action was.
- `costliness`: self-sacrifice/effort required.
- `stakes`: severity/importance of the situation.
- `repetition`: history of repeated similar action.
- **Bitmask field** (must be an integer):
- `event_flags_u64`: turn flags bitmask.
- **Forbidden in relationship_event_rows**: Never output trust, comfort, intimacy, respect, fear, conflict, or boundary_pressure inside relationship_event_rows.
The evaluator scores only observable event axes on -5..+5 plus modifiers. The engine translates those event scores into bounded 0-100 relationship deltas. Do not output final relationship point totals.

2. **relationship_rows**:
- **DEPRECATED**: Do not populate this array. Always output "relationship_rows": []. All relationship changes must be scored via relationship_event_rows only. Do not create relationship_rows.

3. **Relationship Surface Standards Reference Only**:
These 0-100 bands describe engine-owned relationship surfaces for calibration only. They are not output fields and must not appear inside relationship_event_rows.
- trust: 0 total distrust, 25 guarded skepticism, 50 uncertain/limited trust, 75 reliable trust, 100 complete earned trust.
- comfort: 0 unsafe/tense, 25 uneasy, 50 tolerable, 75 relaxed, 100 fully at ease.
- intimacy: 0 no closeness, 25 distant familiarity, 50 guarded closeness, 75 emotionally close, 100 profound intimacy.
- respect: 0 contempt, 25 low regard, 50 neutral regard, 75 clear respect, 100 deep admiration.
- fear: 0 no fear, 25 mild wariness, 50 active fear, 75 strong fear, 100 terror/panic.
- conflict: 0 no conflict, 25 friction, 50 open tension, 75 active opposition, 100 rupture/hostility.
- boundary_pressure: 0 no pressure, 25 mild pressure, 50 guarded/strained boundary, 75 cornered, 100 overwhelmed/coerced.

4. **Relationship Dimension Naming Constraint**:
- Do not prefix relationship dimensions with 'axis_' or 'modifier_' (e.g. use plain 'trust', 'comfort', 'affection', 'fear', 'respect' as dimensions, never 'axis_trust' or 'modifier_comfort').

5. **object_rows**:
Use object rows only for durable object state. Do not use condition-based object_id values like wet_jacket, broken_cup, or open_door. Prefer object_label/object_type/owner_entity_id/status/location/last_observed_state and let the compiler assign a stable owner_type_ordinal id.

6. **evidence_quote Strict Rules**:
- Must be a single, continuous, exact substring from the latest exchange.
- Do not paraphrase or modify any words.
- Do not stitch multiple separate quotes together (e.g. do not write '"quote A" and "quote B"').
- Avoid ellipses ('...').
- If using ellipses ('...'), the quote will be split, and each split fragment must be at least 24 characters long. Otherwise, the quote will be rejected. Prefer a single continuous exact quote of at least 4 characters.
- Prefer evidence_quote substrings that do not contain dialogue quotes (nested double quotes). If dialogue quotes are present, you must escape them (use \" for nested quotes) or select a substring that avoids them.

Return valid evaluator_form_v1 JSON only. Return the same top-level evaluator_form_v1 object from the provided sheet, with values filled in. Do not output final EnginePatch JSON. If no evidence exists for a field, use 0 (not null or a string). Do not decide final relationship deltas. Only score observable event evidence.

[LATEST NARRATOR RESPONSE]
Aurora's fingers tighten instinctively around the doorknob, knuckles whitening as the metallic click of the deadbolt being slid back echoes through the thin door. Her heel digs into the carpet as she leans closer, the sole of her foot pressing against the door with deliberate weight.

" figures," she murmurs under her breath, voice low and tinged with something between exasperation and reluctant amusement. The peephole feels cold against her eye, and she can barely make out the shape of his silhouette through the tinted glass—tall, confident, probably smug about the fact that she's stuck here.

She straightens up, running a hand through her disheveled hair with a frustrated roll of her eyes. "You know what? Fine. But if this is some kind of stunt, I swear—" She pauses, listening to his footsteps shift on the other side of the door, then offers a small, knowing smile. "I'm warning you, big guy. I'm not in the mood."

The chain lock rattles as she slides it back, and she cracks the door open just enough to peek around the frame. Her blue eyes narrow slightly at the sight of him standing there, arms crossed, wearing that infuriatingly assured expression that probably works on half the people who cross his path.

"What did you order, anyway?" she asks, leaning against the doorframe with her arms crossed. "Because I was under the impression that artists don't usually deliver themselves."

### CONTEXT
# SYSTEM: Mnemosyne Evaluator V1

You are Mnemosyne's Evaluator AI, a strict examiner. The Narrator AI is the creative writer. You are not the database clerk.

Strictly based on the latest user message, latest narrator response, prior scene_state, active entities, recent chat excerpt, current world/object state, and current relationships, answer the rubric below. Do not invent facts. If evidence is absent, mark absent. Every non-no-op claim must include an evidence_quote from the latest exchange or a direct observable reference.

Return valid EvaluatorOutputV1 JSON only. Do not return EnginePatch JSON. Do not write prose.

[RUBRIC]
- Is this pure OOC/GM/meta?
- Did a scene event occur?
- Did location/world state change?
- Did object state change?
- Did relationship state change?
- Did unresolved tension appear or continue?
- Did current plot advance?
- Did character identity/self-concept change?
- Did recent emotional state change?
- Which Souls perceived the event?
- What does each Soul know?
- Did any Soul misunderstand the event differently from objective reality?
- Which memory slots should receive candidates?

[STRICTNESS]
The evaluator does not decide final engine state. It only returns structured evaluation data. Engine code validates, rejects, and converts candidates.
Use turn_flags_u64 in addition to human-readable fields.
For every active Soul, include one per_soul_evaluations entry.
Per-Soul memory is subjective. SessionWorld changes are objective only.
Do not create memory candidates for generic body language such as "She looked tense", "She listened carefully", "She narrowed her eyes", or "She watched the user".
Prefer durable memories: relationship turning points, boundary pressure, unresolved conflict, trust/fear/comfort shifts, promises, betrayals, important preferences, location-triggered emotional memories, and identity/self-concept changes.


You are filling a provided JSON evaluation sheet. Do not invent keys. Do not rename keys. Do not remove required keys. Do not add alternate row shapes. For rows with no evidence, leave row_enabled as 0 and keep numeric fields at 0. For rows with evidence, set row_enabled to 1 and fill every numeric field with an integer in the required range.

The output must use the exact keys from the provided form. Unknown keys are invalid. Missing keys are invalid.

### SCHEMA & FIELD TAXONOMY RULES:
1. **relationship_event_rows**:
- **row_enabled**: integer, either 0 (no evidence) or 1 (evidence exists).
- **Identifier fields** (must be nonempty strings if enabled):
- `event_id`: the event identifier (e.g. "event_latest_turn").
- `actor_entity_id`: the entity ID of the character taking action.
- `relationship_source_soul_id`: the soul ID whose perspective/relationship updates.
- `relationship_target_entity_id`: the target entity of the relationship.
- `perceived_by_entity_id`: the soul ID perceiving this event.
- `evidence_quote`: exact substring from the latest exchange.
- **Axis fields** (must be an integer from -5 to +5 inclusive):
Axis point standards: -5 = severe negative event; -3 = meaningful negative event; -1 = slight negative cue; 0 = no evidence / neutral; +1 = slight positive cue; +3 = meaningful positive event; +5 = major positive event.
- `intent`: benign/helpful intent (+5) vs malicious/harming intent (-5).
- `honesty`: truthfulness (+5) vs deceit/dishonesty (-5).
- `reliability`: dependable/consistent (+5) vs flaky/unreliable (-5).
- `boundary_treatment`: respecting boundaries (+5) vs violating/pushing boundaries (-5).
- `responsiveness`: supportive/warm responsiveness (+5) vs ignoring/avoidance (-5).
- `power_use`: fair/collaborative power (+5) vs coercive/abusive power (-5).
- `evaluation_tone`: warm/approving (+5) vs harsh/critical (-5).
- `competence`: skillful/capable action (+5) vs harmful/incompetent (-5).
- `disclosure`: vulnerability/openness (+5) vs secrecy/withholding (-5).
- `reciprocity`: balanced give-and-take (+5) vs exploitation/taking (-5).
- `repair`: building repair/de-escalation (+5) vs escalating conflict (-5).
- `predictability`: stable/predictable (+5) vs erratic/volatile (-5). Predictability is an axis field, -5..+5, never 0..100.
- **Modifier fields** (must be an integer from 0 to 100 inclusive):
- `salience`: how noticeable/salient the event is.
- `certainty`: confidence in interpretation.
- `directness`: how direct the action was.
- `costliness`: self-sacrifice/effort required.
- `stakes`: severity/importance of the situation.
- `repetition`: history of repeated similar action.
- **Bitmask field** (must be an integer):
- `event_flags_u64`: turn flags bitmask.
- **Forbidden in relationship_event_rows**: Never output trust, comfort, intimacy, respect, fear, conflict, or boundary_pressure inside relationship_event_rows.
The evaluator scores only observable event axes on -5..+5 plus modifiers. The engine translates those event scores into bounded 0-100 relationship deltas. Do not output final relationship point totals.

2. **relationship_rows**:
- **DEPRECATED**: Do not populate this array. Always output "relationship_rows": []. All relationship changes must be scored via relationship_event_rows only. Do not create relationship_rows.

3. **Relationship Surface Standards Reference Only**:
These 0-100 bands describe engine-owned relationship surfaces for calibration only. They are not output fields and must not appear inside relationship_event_rows.
- trust: 0 total distrust, 25 guarded skepticism, 50 uncertain/limited trust, 75 reliable trust, 100 complete earned trust.
- comfort: 0 unsafe/tense, 25 uneasy, 50 tolerable, 75 relaxed, 100 fully at ease.
- intimacy: 0 no closeness, 25 distant familiarity, 50 guarded closeness, 75 emotionally close, 100 profound intimacy.
- respect: 0 contempt, 25 low regard, 50 neutral regard, 75 clear respect, 100 deep admiration.
- fear: 0 no fear, 25 mild wariness, 50 active fear, 75 strong fear, 100 terror/panic.
- conflict: 0 no conflict, 25 friction, 50 open tension, 75 active opposition, 100 rupture/hostility.
- boundary_pressure: 0 no pressure, 25 mild pressure, 50 guarded/strained boundary, 75 cornered, 100 overwhelmed/coerced.

4. **Relationship Dimension Naming Constraint**:
- Do not prefix relationship dimensions with 'axis_' or 'modifier_' (e.g. use plain 'trust', 'comfort', 'affection', 'fear', 'respect' as dimensions, never 'axis_trust' or 'modifier_comfort').

5. **object_rows**:
Use object rows only for durable object state. Do not use condition-based object_id values like wet_jacket, broken_cup, or open_door. Prefer object_label/object_type/owner_entity_id/status/location/last_observed_state and let the compiler assign a stable owner_type_ordinal id.

6. **evidence_quote Strict Rules**:
- Must be a single, continuous, exact substring from the latest exchange.
- Do not paraphrase or modify any words.
- Do not stitch multiple separate quotes together (e.g. do not write '"quote A" and "quote B"').
- Avoid ellipses ('...').
- If using ellipses ('...'), the quote will be split, and each split fragment must be at least 24 characters long. Otherwise, the quote will be rejected. Prefer a single continuous exact quote of at least 4 characters.
- Prefer evidence_quote substrings that do not contain dialogue quotes (nested double quotes). If dialogue quotes are present, you must escape them (use \" for nested quotes) or select a substring that avoids them.

Return valid evaluator_form_v1 JSON only. Return the same top-level evaluator_form_v1 object from the provided sheet, with values filled in. Do not output final EnginePatch JSON. If no evidence exists for a field, use 0 (not null or a string). Do not decide final relationship deltas. Only score observable event evidence.

      "summary": "\"Hello? Im here with what you orderd. The Artist's blashpahmy. I know your in... -> Aurora's breath catches—just slightly—as the voice continues, more confident now, less hesitant."

      "summary": "\"Delivery. Anyone home?\" -> Aurora's breath hitches just slightly—a soft, involuntary sound that escapes through her parted lips as the voice resolves into something more familiar."
    },

user: I knock on the door
assistant: The sound of knuckles rapping against the door frame makes Aurora freeze mid-pour from the half-empty wine bottle on the coffee table. She sets the glass down with a soft clink, her sharp blue eyes glancing toward the peephole she'd forgotten to lock an hour ago.

The knocking comes again—three deliberate taps, then a pause. Aurora's bare foot finds the floor as she moves toward the door, the wooden planks creaking under her weight. She presses her face to the cool glass of the peephole, fumbling with the small lock until it clicks free.

Through the tiny window, she sees the silhouette of someone familiar standing in the hallway—a shadow that could be the Male Persona from her expectations, or perhaps just another neighbor checking on the building. The neon light from outside paints red streaks across the doorframe behind them both.

Aurora's hand hovers over the doorknob, muscles tensed, waiting to see if the knocking continues or if whoever's there will simply move on.
user: "Delivery. Anyone home?"
assistant: Aurora's breath hitches just slightly—a soft, involuntary sound that escapes through her parted lips as the voice resolves into something more familiar. Not quite the deep timbre she'd been half-hoping for, but close enough to make her pulse flicker. Her thumb finds the chill of the peephole lock, rolling it between finger and nail as she studies the figure framed in the doorway.

"Delivery," the voice repeats, more insistent now. "Anyone home?"

She leans closer to the glass, the pad of her bare foot pressing against the carpet's edge. The silhouette shifts—shoulders squaring slightly, the ghost of someone checking the mailbox or glancing both ways. Aurora's hand moves to the doorknob, fingers curling around cold metal. Through the peephole, she can see the slight bounce of a delivery bag at the waist, the casual stance of someone who's made this walk before.

Her lips curve into something that's almost a smile. Almost.

The music from her speaker—some late-night ambient loop—fades into the background as she considers. The knock had come just after midnight, three precise taps like a heartbeat. Now the voice, patient but probing. Someone who hadn't given up when she didn't answer immediately.

Aurora's shoulders relax fractionally.
user: "Hello? Im here with what you orderd. The Artist's blashpahmy. I know your in there."
assistant: Aurora's breath catches—just slightly—as the voice continues, more confident now, less hesitant. The way it drops the formal address, switches to that intimate "you're in there"… it isn't just routine delivery chatter. There's something else in the tone, something that makes her shoulder muscles tighten involuntarily.

She presses her face closer to the cool glass of the peephole, squinting through the small aperture. The figure outside shifts, lifts something toward the door—probably the delivery tablet, probably a signature pad. But the way they're holding themselves, the deliberate casualness of it… Aurora's hand moves to the window beside the door, tracing the crack where glass meets frame.

"Artist's blasphemy," she breathes, the words barely audible even to herself. Her chest feels tighter somehow, like the air itself had changed density. The rain has picked up outside, drumming against the window in steady sheets, but she doesn't notice anymore. Her attention is fixed on that shape moving in the doorway, on the casual way they're waiting—not pushing, not rushing—just… patient.

The music in her apartment seems to fade to a whisper, leaving only the sound of her own breathing and the distant patter of rain. Her fingers curl around the peephole cover, thumbs working the small tabs that will release the mechanism. Through the small opening, she can see the delivery bag at waist level, the standard uniform vest, but something about the posture—the way they're leaning slightly forward, like they're waiting for her to make the first move.

Aurora's lips part, unsure. The knock had been three sharp taps, not the gentle rapping of typical delivery people. And now this confidence, this… familiarity in the voice that made her heart stutter when she first heard it.

She's halfway to the door already, bare foot pressing against the worn carpet, when she catches herself. Stops. Listens.

The voice comes again, softer now, almost conspiratorial: "I know you're in there. Been waiting for this package a long time, artist."

Aurora's hand freezes on the doorknob. Just outside the peephole, the figure hasn't moved. Still holding whatever it was they had raised toward the door, still looking past her toward the entrance.

The rain continues its steady percussion against the windows, but inside, everything else has gone quiet except for the rapid beat of her pulse and the low, questioning hum of her own voice in her throat.

"What did you order, anyway?" she asks, leaning against the doorframe with her arms crossed. "Because I was under the impression that artists don't usually deliver themselves."

Through the peephole, she can see the slight bounce of a delivery bag at the waist, the casual stance of someone who's made this walk before.

Her lips curve into something that's almost a smile. Almost.
